It is painful to learn that the most polite people In the world are still be¬ lievers la devil control, still unversed in conventional morality, but it Is a pain made rather exquisite when the recital of the shortcomings of the K.skimos of Greenland comes from the pen of Lieutenant FrrziiroH Gbeev, U. S. X., of the Crocker Land expe¬ dition, whose articles on “Arctic Duty” are running in' the Naval In¬ stitute Proceedings. Lieutenant Green neither condones nor condemns, but looks upon the brown children of the north with an admirable and kindly phlIo.sophy. He seem.s to undersUiidl bow naturally the natives' cnnie to tliat animistic religion which provlde.s separate de¬ mons for tlio ice, (he .sea, the -tt'lads, the fiord. The ChrlKtlan missionaries have been unable to eradicate the old belief, and tlie writer doe.s not criti¬ cise them for their failure, but rather nppinud.s them for their untiring, un¬ selfish work to spread the doctrine of brotherly love among live Eskimos. Green hini.self finds one of the causes of tlic .superstitions the deceptive light of the far north. He does not mention it, but this very thing, re¬ sulting in optical illusion, made Peart see a Crocker Land that did not ex¬ ist, and incidentally .sent Green on tile errand of which he writes so well. The E.skjmo may well see devils when an American naval Lieutenant Is thus affected; “As the twilight sets !n, a vague un¬ easiness sometimes comes. We -white men felt it; it is a purely psychological phenomenon, a kind of anxiety psychosis of fatigue, I imagine. Shades glide across the way. Grotesque figures beckon silently on the moor, -which Is gray white, like dteth. Or they hover at a little distance. In a kind of dread¬ ful hesitation os it undecided whether to pursue or not. Thus It is the native meets dead friends, personified deeds, manlike animals, or any other of the In¬ numerable forms that He within the power of oil superior devils. Nor does the ghastly show stop here. Familiar landmarks rear unsteadily, suddenly cx pand with catastrophic violence, and having submitted to utter annihilation sink Inaudibly hack to their original shape and mass. Stars fixed forever In the memory of man tear madly across the flaming Arctic heavens; and, having mated In all their blazing grandeur, re¬ turn ewlftly to their accustomed nlchea “Miniq swore to me that he had seen Arcturus twice on such a Jaunt. Skjsu, a man of the highest character,- my foster father and enduring friend, wit¬ nessed the brilliant descent of a well kno-wm planet, which dipped Into the sea near Kookan. Nay, ho even heard the spitting steam -which rose in clouds wherein the star’s return was concealed. These eyes that guide my pen fas a matt^ of unliterary fact they are play¬ ing "Over There' on a rusty Corona) have scanned the dreary snow plateaus above Etah until, in sheer vexation at the immobility of all thinga some re¬ bellious nerve within became hypersensltlvely apt. A black creature romps across the little valley at my feet. As I hesitate, halt startled, it returns. Then again aro:u/d the eombre vUta; and again returns. Yet It Is only a rook, a single black boulder face that has been bared by the wind acd stands out sharply black amid the boundleas gray white afternoon. "Oh, it's well, friend reader, to ridi¬ cule the poor barbarian’s Almighty Devil. But wait: -who knows, some day In Hero Land perhaps you too may bo tired, agonizingly fatigued, -weary be¬ yond all power of pain to choke curses from your withered throat. Then, take the word of one who knows, you’ll see the AlmighI)’ Devil and ail his court. And you’ll gasp a final thanksgiving If they pass unseeing." The Eskimo.? that Green ■visited, and he xvenk to many of the Danish settioments, have no morals in matters of the marital relation, yet one of their variou.s devils pijvents them from permitting children from per .sons too closely related. "Tliey have no form of marriage, and (heir court¬ ship.? are odd: “Instead of 'wooing the affection of the maiden the .suitor practises all his little parlor tricks on the father, taking care to make it plain that-, no other young fellow in the neighborhood Is so clever with harpoon and whip, and none so well disposed to support aged In-laws when famine seizes upon the weak and mutilates the strong. In one or two cases I knew' of the lucky man gained his point, and bride, by a .suitable gift of dogs, or line, or skins and meat, to her father. In other -words, he simply was the highest bidder—which, barring the impulse of animal emotion. Is what even conventional marriage amounts to. Roman countrymen, am I not correct?"
